Task

Edit tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$470, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$474, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$478, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$482, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$486, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$490, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$494, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$498, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$502, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$506, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$510, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$514, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$518, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$522, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$526, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$530, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$368(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$371(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$374(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$377(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$380, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$383, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$386, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$389, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$392, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$395, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$398, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$401, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$404, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$407, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$410, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$413. The plan names their shared sum as 'NONE (no converted product shares a sum with another; each activation block carries its own intercept on bx, each norm block has no addend)'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
  • The sum these products feed also adds terms that are not products: data_intercept_delayed.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$470, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$474, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$478, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$482, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$486, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$490, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$494, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$498, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$502, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$506, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$510, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$514, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$518, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$522, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$526, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$530, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$368(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$371(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$374(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$377(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$380, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$383, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$386, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$389, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$392, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$395, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$398, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$401, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$404, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$407, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$410, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$413
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (no converted product shares a sum with another; each activation block carries its own intercept on bx, each norm block has no addend)
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING: 16 blocks in activation (one per lane i = 0..15): clk=clk, reset=act_blk_reset, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0, inp_data_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, ay={11'b0, data_slope_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, bx={28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result[7:0] -> intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8 +: 8] (now a wire), chainout and scanout unconnected. 16 blocks in norm (one per lane i = 0..15): clk=clk, reset=norm_blk_reset, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0, (validity_mask[i] ? (inp_data[i*8 +: 8] - mean) : inp_data[i*8 +: 8])}, ay=(validity_mask[i] ? {11'b0, inv_var} : 19'd1), bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result[7:0] -> variance_applied_data[i*8 +: 8] (now a wire), chainout and scanout unconnected.
PLAN:

What is converted

32 blocks total, 32 of the 132 DSP tiles. No chaining: every chainin is tied to 64'b0 and every chainout is left unconnected.

A. activation, the 16 tanh lanes (ids $470 … $530) — the full mult_add goes into one block each

Source (lines 5450–5458, 5465–5534). Per lane i, with P_k = inp_data_flopped[i*8+:8] after edge k, Q_k = data_slope_flopped[i*8+:8] after edge k, J_k = data_intercept_flopped[i*8+:8] after edge k, the original computes, while running with activation_type==1:

  • edge k: slope_applied_data_internal[i] <= Q_{k-1} * P_{k-1} (line 5482), data_intercept_flopped[i] <= data_intercept[i] (5483), data_intercept_delayed[i] <= J_{k-1} (5484)
  • edge k: intercept_applied_data_internal[i] <= slope_applied(k-1) + data_intercept_delayed(k-1) = P_{k-2}*Q_{k-2} + J_{k-2} (5485), all in 8 bits, i.e. modulo 256.

The block, fed ax = inp_data_flopped[i], ay = data_slope_flopped[i], bx = data_intercept_flopped[i], registers those at edge k-1 (holding P_{k-2}, Q_{k-2}, J_{k-2}) and loads result at edge k with P_{k-2}*Q_{k-2} + J_{k-2}. result[7:0] is exactly intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8+:8] — the block's low 8 result bits are the same modulo-256 value the 8-bit design registers hold, since each operand is zero-extended and the design truncates at the same point.

So the block's operand registers take over the roles of slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed, and the block's result register takes over intercept_applied_data_internal. Those three register arrays are deleted. data_intercept_flopped stays in soft logic exactly as written (it is the block's bx source), as do inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped, relu_applied_data_internal, cycle_count, activation_in_progress, done_activation_internal, out_data_available_internal, the slope/intercept LUTs and the address logic.

Visibility. slope_applied_data_internal is read only at line 5485 and data_intercept_delayed only at 5485; neither leaves the module, so both may live inside the block. intercept_applied_data_internal is read at 5534 only, and it is produced on the block's result port, so it stays visible.

Reset. act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress), the exact complement of the load condition of the always block at 5465–5520 (if (reset || ~enable_activation) clears, else if (in_data_available || activation_in_progress) loads, else clears). On a cycle where the design clears slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed and intercept_applied_data_internal to 0 (5467/5468/5470 and 5522/5523/5525), the block clears its operand registers, its chainin register and result to 0 — and on the first loading edge after such a clear the block computes 0*0+0 = 0, matching the design's 0 + 0 = 0. A clear of reset alone also clears inp_data_flopped/data_slope_flopped (5451–5453) one stage upstream; that is unaffected, those registers stay soft.

B. norm, the 16 lanes (ids $368 … $413) — the multiply plus its two register stages go into one block each

Source (lines 5049–5107). Per lane i, with d_k = (inp_data[i*8+:8] - mean) just before edge k:

  • edge k: mean_applied_data[i] <= mask[i] ? d_k : inp_data[i] (5069/5073)
  • edge k: variance_applied_data[i] <= mask[i] ? (mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var) : mean_applied_data[i] (5070/5074), 8 bits.

The block's ax register takes over mean_applied_data[i] and its result register takes over variance_applied_data[i]; the subtraction stays in soft logic in front of ax, and the mask choice on the multiply is carried on ay (inv_var when the lane is valid, constant 1 when it is not, so mean_applied * 1 = mean_applied in the low 8 bits — the pass-through of 5074). bx and chainin are tied to 0: this product has no addend in norm.

Visibility. mean_applied_data is read only at lines 5070 and 5074 inside the same always block, so it may live inside the block as the ax register. variance_applied_data is read at 5107 (out_data_internal, hence out_data, hence pool — including the four lanes whose bytes feed pool's four-term window-4 adder at line 5392), and it is produced on the block's result port, so it stays visible. The four (sum_in=top) lanes are converted as products only; their shared four-term sum stays where it is, in soft logic, unchanged — a four-term sum of four distinct products cannot be folded into one block's single bx/chainin, and chainout may not reach general routing.

Reset. norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress), the exact complement of the load condition at 5050/5059. norm never holds these two register arrays: every branch of the always block either loads them (5069/5070/5073/5074) or clears both to 0 (5051/5052 and 5098/5099), so the block's synchronous clear reproduces the register behaviour exactly, including after a mid-run reset. On the first loading edge after a clear, the block computes 0*ay + 0 = 0, matching variance_applied_data <= 0 * inv_var.

Power-up. Both block instances are held in reset through the testbench's 4-cycle reset phase (reset is high, so *_blk_reset is high), so every block register is cleared at the first clock edge and no X reaches an output on or after the first compared cycle. No rewrite relies on a block result before its second edge after power-up.

Candidates left unconverted
  • qmult.$mul$...4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances — not chosen this pass; not implementable on this device. The region is 256 copies of the PE multiplier (one per seq_mac, line 4728 through processing_element), and one block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone, so converting the region would need 256 tiles against the device's 132. Converting only part of the region would still spend up to 132 tiles to remove at most half of the PE multipliers, which is why no partial conversion is proposed either. (The product itself would map: a/b reach mul_out_temp_reg through exactly two register stages, 4683–4693, matching the block's two stages; but the accumulation out_temp <= out_temp + mul_out_temp_reg at 4697–4704 is a feedback loop through soft logic that cannot enter the block, since chainin may only be driven by another block's chainout or a constant. So even with tiles to spare, only the multiply, not the accumulate, could move.)
  • No other candidate is left: all 16 activation ids and all 16 norm ids are converted.
Register stages, before and after
  • Each activation id: before — 2 stages from the product's operands (inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped, and the matching data_intercept_flopped) to the sum result intercept_applied_data_internal: slope_applied_data_internal/data_intercept_delayed (stage 1), intercept_applied_data_internal (stage 2). After — 2 stages: the block's operand registers (stage 1), the block's result register (stage 2). Unchanged latency, and the stage upstream (inp_data_flopped/data_slope_flopped) is untouched.
  • Each norm id: before — 2 stages from inp_data/mean/inv_var to variance_applied_data: mean_applied_data (stage 1), variance_applied_data (stage 2). After — 2 stages: the block's operand registers (stage 1, holding the already-subtracted lane value), the block's result register (stage 2). Unchanged latency.
Edit notes for the editor

In norm: delete the mean_applied_data and variance_applied_data reg declarations (5023–5024) and every assignment to them (5051–5052, 5069–5070, 5073–5074, 5098–5099 — the for loop body at 5067–5076 becomes empty and the loop may go with it); declare wire [DESIGN_SIZE*DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data; so that line 5107 still works; add wire norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress); and a generate loop of 16 mult_add_int_18x19 instances wired as in MAPPING, driving assign variance_applied_data[gi*8 +: 8] = res[7:0];. Leave cycle_count, norm_in_progress, done_norm_internal, out_data_available_internal, in_data_available_flopped, inp_data_flopped and the enable/mux logic untouched.

In activation: delete the slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed declarations (5433, 5444) and every assignment to them (5467, 5470, 5482, 5484, 5522, 5525); change intercept_applied_data_internal from reg to wire and delete its assignments (5468, 5485, 5523). Keep line 5483 (data_intercept_flopped <= data_intercept) and the data_intercept_flopped clears at 5471 and 5526 as they are — the tanh branch of the for loop keeps only that one statement, the ReLU branch (5487) is untouched. Add wire act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress); and a generate loop of 16 mult_add_int_18x19 instances wired as in MAPPING, driving assign intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*8 +: 8] = res[7:0];. Line 5534 and everything else stay as written.

Connect every input port by name on all 32 instances, including mode_sigs(12'b0), scanin(19'b0) and chainin(64'b0); leave chainout() and scanout() unconnected.

ASSUMES: inv_var and validity_mask are stable while norm is processing a matrix. The block registers ay at the same edge as ax (one edge before result), so the multiplier it applies is inv_var/validity_mask[i] sampled one cycle earlier than the design samples them at line 5070, where they are read combinationally at the edge that loads variance_applied_data. They agree whenever the two signals do not change during a run. Support in the design: both come from cfg registers (inv_var written only at REG_INV_VAR_ADDR, line 4889; validity_mask_a_rows only at REG_VALID_MASK_A_ROWS_ADDR, line 4894) that top wires straight to norm (lines 5897, 5902), while norm only processes between in_data_available/norm_in_progress (line 5059) and done_norm_internal (5090), a window control enters only after start_tpu and done_mat_mul (lines 5259, 5271, 5276) — i.e. after software has finished configuring. Outside that window norm_blk_reset is high and every block register is 0, exactly as the design's registers are (5051–5052, 5098–5099), so the sampling offset is invisible there.

ASSUMES: activation_type is stable while activation is enabled and processing. When activation_type==0 the design holds slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed and intercept_applied_data_internal (the for loop at 5480–5489 assigns only relu_applied_data_internal in that case), while the block has no clock enable and keeps loading. The held values are only ever read through out_data_internal when activation_type==1 (line 5534), so the two differ only if activation_type changes from 1 to 0 and back within one activation run. Support in the design: activation_type is a cfg register written only at REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR (line 4913) and reset to 1 (line 4838), and activation only runs between in_data_available/activation_in_progress (5477) and done_activation_internal (5505/5513), a window control enters only after start_tpu and the earlier stages complete (5259, 5319). Outside that window act_blk_reset is high and every block register is 0, matching the design's clears at 5467–5469 and 5522–5524, so the missing hold is invisible there. Note the block's reset is deliberately not gated by activation_type: leaving it ungated keeps the 0→1 transition exact (the design loads a fresh product at that edge, and so does the block).

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
// inputs).
// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
// X until the first clear.
module mult_add_int_18x19 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
    input [17:0] ax,
    input [18:0] ay,
    input [35:0] bx,
    input [63:0] chainin,
    input [18:0] scanin,
    output reg [63:0] result,
    output [63:0] chainout,
    output [18:0] scanout
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            result <= 0;
            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
        end
    end

    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
    assign scanout  = ay_reg;

endmodule

////////////////////////////////////////////////
// Normalization block
////////////////////////////////////////////////

module norm(
    input enable_norm,
    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean,
    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] inv_var,
    input in_data_available,
    input [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data,
    output [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data,
    output out_data_available,
    input [`MASK_WIDTH-1:0] validity_mask,
    output done_norm,
    input clk,
    input reset
);

reg out_data_available_internal;
wire [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data_internal;
reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] mean_applied_data;
reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data;
reg done_norm_internal;
reg norm_in_progress;
reg in_data_available_flopped;
reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data_flopped;

//Muxing logic to handle the case when this block is disabled
assign out_data_available = (enable_norm) ? out_data_available_internal : in_data_available_flopped;
assign out_data = (enable_norm) ? out_data_internal : inp_data_flopped;
assign done_norm = (enable_norm) ? done_norm_internal : 1'b1;

//inp_data will have multiple elements in it. the number of elements is the same as size of the matmul.
//on each clock edge, if in_data_available is 1, then we will normalize the inputs.

//the code uses the funky part-select syntax. example:
//wire [7:0] byteN = word[byte_num*8 +: 8];
//byte_num*8 is the starting point. 8 is the width is the part-select (has to be constant).in_data_available
//+: indicates the part-select increases from the starting point
//-: indicates the part-select decreases from the starting point
//another example:
//loc = 3;
//PA[loc -:4] = PA[loc+1 +:4];  // equivalent to PA[3:0] = PA[7:4];

reg [31:0] cycle_count;
reg [31:0] i;
always @(posedge clk) begin
    if ((reset || ~enable_norm)) begin
        mean_applied_data <= 0;
        variance_applied_data <= 0;
        out_data_available_internal <= 0;
        cycle_count <= 0;
        done_norm_internal <= 0;
        norm_in_progress <= 0;
        in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available;
        inp_data_flopped <= inp_data;
    end else if (in_data_available || norm_in_progress) begin
        cycle_count <= cycle_count + 1;
        //Let's apply mean and variance as the input data comes in.
        //We have a pipeline here. First stage does the add (to apply the mean)
        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).
        //Note: the following loop is not a loop across multiple columns of data.
        //This loop will run in 2 cycle on the same column of data that comes into 
        //this module in 1 clock.
        for (i = 0; i < `DESIGN_SIZE; i=i+1) begin
            if (validity_mask[i] == 1'b1) begin
                mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (inp_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] - mean);
                variance_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] * inv_var);
            end 
            else begin
                mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (inp_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]);
                variance_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]);
            end
        end

        //Out data is available starting with the second clock cycle because 
        //in the first cycle, we only apply the mean.
        if(cycle_count==2) begin
            out_data_available_internal <= 1;
        end

        //When we've normalized values N times, where N is the matmul
        //size, that means we're done. But there is one additional cycle
        //that is taken in the beginning (when we are applying the mean to the first
        //column of data). We can call this the Initiation Interval of the pipeline.
        //So, for a 4x4 matmul, this block takes 5 cycles.
        if(cycle_count==(`DESIGN_SIZE+1)) be

////////////////////////////////////////////////
// Activation block
////////////////////////////////////////////////

module activation(
    input activation_type,
    input enable_activation,
    input in_data_available,
    input [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data,
    output [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data,
    output out_data_available,
    input [`MASK_WIDTH-1:0] validity_mask,
    output done_activation,
    input clk,
    input reset
);

reg  done_activation_internal;
reg  out_data_available_internal;
wire [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data_internal;
reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] slope_applied_data_internal;
reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] intercept_applied_data_internal;
reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] relu_applied_data_internal;
reg [31:0] i;
reg [31:0] cycle_count;
reg activation_in_progress;

reg [(`DESIGN_SIZE*4)-1:0] address;
reg [(`DESIGN_SIZE*8)-1:0] data_slope;
reg [(`DESIGN_SIZE*8)-1:0] data_slope_flopped;
reg [(`DESIGN_SIZE*8)-1:0] data_intercept;
reg [(`DESIGN_SIZE*8)-1:0] data_intercept_delayed;
reg [(`DESIGN_SIZE*8)-1:0] data_intercept_flopped;

reg in_data_available_flopped;
reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data_flopped;

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    inp_data_flopped <= 0;
    data_slope_flopped <= 0;
  end else begin
    inp_data_flopped <= inp_data;
    data_slope_flopped <= data_slope;
  end
end

// If the activation block is not enabled, just forward the input data
assign out_data             = enable_activation ? out_data_internal : inp_data_flopped;
assign done_activation      = enable_activation ? done_activation_internal : 1'b1;
assign out_data_available   = enable_activation ? out_data_available_internal : in_data_available_flopped;

always @(posedge clk) begin
   if (reset || ~enable_activation) begin
      slope_applied_data_internal     <= 0;
      intercept_applied_data_internal <= 0; 
      relu_applied_data_internal      <= 0; 
      data_intercept_delayed      <= 0;
      data_intercept_flopped      <= 0;
      done_activation_internal    <= 0;
      out_data_available_internal <= 0;
      cycle_count                 <= 0;
      activation_in_progress      <= 0;
      in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available;
   end else if(in_data_available || activation_in_progress) begin
      cycle_count <= cycle_count + 1;

      for (i = 0; i < `DESIGN_SIZE; i=i+1) begin
         if(activation_type==1'b1) begin // tanH
            slope_applied_data_internal[i*`DWIDTH +:`DWIDTH] <= data_slope_flopped[i*8 +: 8] * inp_data_flopped[i*`DWIDTH +:`DWIDTH];
            data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8] <= data_intercept[i*8 +: 8];
            data_intercept_delayed[i*8 +: 8] <= data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8];
            intercept_applied_data_internal[i*`DWIDTH +:`DWIDTH] <= slope_applied_data_internal[i*`DWIDTH +:`DWIDTH] + data_intercept_delayed[i*8 +: 8];
         end else begin // ReLU
            relu_applied_data_internal[i*`DWIDTH +:`DWIDTH] <= inp_data[i*`DWIDTH] ? {`DWIDTH{1'b0}} : inp_data[i*`DWIDTH +:`DWIDTH];
         end
      end   

      //TANH needs 1 extra cycle
      if (activation_type==1'b1) begin
         if (cycle_count==3) begin
            out_data_available_internal <= 1;
         end
      end else begin
         if (cycle_count==2) begin
           out_data_available_internal <= 1;
         end
      end

      //TANH needs 1 extra cycle
      if (activation_type==1'b1) begin
        if(cycle_count==(`DESIGN_SIZE+2)) begin
           done_activation_internal <= 1'b1;
           activation_in_progress <= 0;
        end
        else begin
           activation_in_progress <= 1;

{

output
        // tpu_like.small.os.fixed at its top: the ws drive sequence on the os register map and its stride-16
        // layout. Run 1 matmul alone; the APB write that pulses pe_reset (the design's own one-cycle pulse,
        // with the array quiet: the protocol); a full reset; run 2 every block; a full reset; run 3 matmul
        // and norm; the random phase (APB traffic on the register set except the start/done word, BRAM
        // writes, reset 1 in 1024). Under START_ONLY the full resets and the random resets are left out.
        // Under PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT (the diagnostic) one more APB write of the pe_reset bit lands while run
        // 2's multiply is in flight, which the protocol excludes.
        fill_matrices;
        phase = "run1";
        load_matrices(0, 10'd0, 10'd0);
        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
        apb_write(8'h00, 32'h1); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
        phase = "readback1"; read_c(10'd768, "run 1 (matmul)");
        phase = "pe_reset"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // the pe_reset bit, array quiet
        zero_cycles(4);
        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
        phase = "run2";
        load_matrices(1, 10'd256, 10'd256);
        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
        config_blocks(32'hf); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
`ifdef PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT
        zero_cycles(30); phase = "pe_reset-live"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // a one-cycle pe_reset with products in flight
`endif
        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
        phase = "readback2"; read_c(10'd768, "run 2 (every block)");
        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
        phase = "run3";
        load_matrices(2, 10'd512, 10'd512);
        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
        config_blocks(32'h3); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
        phase = "readback3"; read_c(10'd768, "run 3 (matmul and norm, after reset)");
        phase = "random"; ph = 0;
        for (i = 0; i < `RANDOM_CYCLES; i = i + 1) begin
            tick; set_data;
            case (ph)
                0: begin PSEL = 1; draw; PWRITE = r32[0]; PADDR = reg_addr(r32[5:1]); if (PADDR == 8'h04) PADDR = 8'h00; PENABLE = 0; apb_addr = PADDR; apb_write_q = PWRITE; apb_wdata = PWDATA; end   // no random start or pe_reset write: the protocol
                1: begin PSEL = 1; PENABLE = 1; PADDR = apb_addr; PWRITE = apb_write_q; PWDATA = apb_wdata; end   // set_data redrew them; the access cycle keeps the setup cycle's (APB: stable through setup and access)
                2: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; end
                default: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; end
            endcase
            ph = (ph + 1) % 4;
            draw; bram_we_a_ext = (r32[1:0] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0; bram_we_b_ext = (r32[3:2] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0;
            draw; reset = (`MIDRUN_RESETS && (r32 % 1024) == 0); resetn = ~reset;
        end
        PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; bram_we_a_ext = 0; bram_we_b_ext = 0;

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.

The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$470, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$474, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$478, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$482, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$486, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$490, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$494, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$498, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$502, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$506, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$510, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$514, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$518, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$522, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$526, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$530, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$368(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$371(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$374(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$377(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$380, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$383, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$386, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$389, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$392, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$395, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$398, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$401, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$404, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$407, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$410, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$413
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (no converted product shares a sum with another; each activation block carries its own intercept on bx, each norm block has no addend)
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING: 16 blocks in activation (one per lane i = 0..15): clk=clk, reset=act_blk_reset, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0, inp_data_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, ay={11'b0, data_slope_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, bx={28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result[7:0] -> intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8 +: 8] (now a wire), chainout and scanout unconnected. 16 blocks in norm (one per lane i = 0..15): clk=clk, reset=norm_blk_reset, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0, (validity_mask[i] ? (inp_data[i*8 +: 8] - mean) : inp_data[i*8 +: 8])}, ay=(validity_mask[i] ? {11'b0, inv_var} : 19'd1), bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result[7:0] -> variance_applied_data[i*8 +: 8] (now a wire), chainout and scanout unconnected.
PLAN:

What is converted

32 blocks total, 32 of the 132 DSP tiles. No chaining: every chainin is tied to 64'b0 and every chainout is left unconnected.

A. activation, the 16 tanh lanes (ids $470 … $530) — the full mult_add goes into one block each

Source (lines 5450–5458, 5465–5534). Per lane i, with P_k = inp_data_flopped[i*8+:8] after edge k, Q_k = data_slope_flopped[i*8+:8] after edge k, J_k = data_intercept_flopped[i*8+:8] after edge k, the original computes, while running with activation_type==1:

  • edge k: slope_applied_data_internal[i] <= Q_{k-1} * P_{k-1} (line 5482), data_intercept_flopped[i] <= data_intercept[i] (5483), data_intercept_delayed[i] <= J_{k-1} (5484)
  • edge k: intercept_applied_data_internal[i] <= slope_applied(k-1) + data_intercept_delayed(k-1) = P_{k-2}*Q_{k-2} + J_{k-2} (5485), all in 8 bits, i.e. modulo 256.

The block, fed ax = inp_data_flopped[i], ay = data_slope_flopped[i], bx = data_intercept_flopped[i], registers those at edge k-1 (holding P_{k-2}, Q_{k-2}, J_{k-2}) and loads result at edge k with P_{k-2}*Q_{k-2} + J_{k-2}. result[7:0] is exactly intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8+:8] — the block's low 8 result bits are the same modulo-256 value the 8-bit design registers hold, since each operand is zero-extended and the design truncates at the same point.

So the block's operand registers take over the roles of slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed, and the block's result register takes over intercept_applied_data_internal. Those three register arrays are deleted. data_intercept_flopped stays in soft logic exactly as written (it is the block's bx source), as do inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped, relu_applied_data_internal, cycle_count, activation_in_progress, done_activation_internal, out_data_available_internal, the slope/intercept LUTs and the address logic.

Visibility. slope_applied_data_internal is read only at line 5485 and data_intercept_delayed only at 5485; neither leaves the module, so both may live inside the block. intercept_applied_data_internal is read at 5534 only, and it is produced on the block's result port, so it stays visible.

Reset. act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress), the exact complement of the load condition of the always block at 5465–5520 (if (reset || ~enable_activation) clears, else if (in_data_available || activation_in_progress) loads, else clears). On a cycle where the design clears slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed and intercept_applied_data_internal to 0 (5467/5468/5470 and 5522/5523/5525), the block clears its operand registers, its chainin register and result to 0 — and on the first loading edge after such a clear the block computes 0*0+0 = 0, matching the design's 0 + 0 = 0. A clear of reset alone also clears inp_data_flopped/data_slope_flopped (5451–5453) one stage upstream; that is unaffected, those registers stay soft.

B. norm, the 16 lanes (ids $368 … $413) — the multiply plus its two register stages go into one block each

Source (lines 5049–5107). Per lane i, with d_k = (inp_data[i*8+:8] - mean) just before edge k:

  • edge k: mean_applied_data[i] <= mask[i] ? d_k : inp_data[i] (5069/5073)
  • edge k: variance_applied_data[i] <= mask[i] ? (mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var) : mean_applied_data[i] (5070/5074), 8 bits.

The block's ax register takes over mean_applied_data[i] and its result register takes over variance_applied_data[i]; the subtraction stays in soft logic in front of ax, and the mask choice on the multiply is carried on ay (inv_var when the lane is valid, constant 1 when it is not, so mean_applied * 1 = mean_applied in the low 8 bits — the pass-through of 5074). bx and chainin are tied to 0: this product has no addend in norm.

Visibility. mean_applied_data is read only at lines 5070 and 5074 inside the same always block, so it may live inside the block as the ax register. variance_applied_data is read at 5107 (out_data_internal, hence out_data, hence pool — including the four lanes whose bytes feed pool's four-term window-4 adder at line 5392), and it is produced on the block's result port, so it stays visible. The four (sum_in=top) lanes are converted as products only; their shared four-term sum stays where it is, in soft logic, unchanged — a four-term sum of four distinct products cannot be folded into one block's single bx/chainin, and chainout may not reach general routing.

Reset. norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress), the exact complement of the load condition at 5050/5059. norm never holds these two register arrays: every branch of the always block either loads them (5069/5070/5073/5074) or clears both to 0 (5051/5052 and 5098/5099), so the block's synchronous clear reproduces the register behaviour exactly, including after a mid-run reset. On the first loading edge after a clear, the block computes 0*ay + 0 = 0, matching variance_applied_data <= 0 * inv_var.

Power-up. Both block instances are held in reset through the testbench's 4-cycle reset phase (reset is high, so *_blk_reset is high), so every block register is cleared at the first clock edge and no X reaches an output on or after the first compared cycle. No rewrite relies on a block result before its second edge after power-up.

Candidates left unconverted
  • qmult.$mul$...4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances — not chosen this pass; not implementable on this device. The region is 256 copies of the PE multiplier (one per seq_mac, line 4728 through processing_element), and one block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone, so converting the region would need 256 tiles against the device's 132. Converting only part of the region would still spend up to 132 tiles to remove at most half of the PE multipliers, which is why no partial conversion is proposed either. (The product itself would map: a/b reach mul_out_temp_reg through exactly two register stages, 4683–4693, matching the block's two stages; but the accumulation out_temp <= out_temp + mul_out_temp_reg at 4697–4704 is a feedback loop through soft logic that cannot enter the block, since chainin may only be driven by another block's chainout or a constant. So even with tiles to spare, only the multiply, not the accumulate, could move.)
  • No other candidate is left: all 16 activation ids and all 16 norm ids are converted.
Register stages, before and after
  • Each activation id: before — 2 stages from the product's operands (inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped, and the matching data_intercept_flopped) to the sum result intercept_applied_data_internal: slope_applied_data_internal/data_intercept_delayed (stage 1), intercept_applied_data_internal (stage 2). After — 2 stages: the block's operand registers (stage 1), the block's result register (stage 2). Unchanged latency, and the stage upstream (inp_data_flopped/data_slope_flopped) is untouched.
  • Each norm id: before — 2 stages from inp_data/mean/inv_var to variance_applied_data: mean_applied_data (stage 1), variance_applied_data (stage 2). After — 2 stages: the block's operand registers (stage 1, holding the already-subtracted lane value), the block's result register (stage 2). Unchanged latency.
Edit notes for the editor

In norm: delete the mean_applied_data and variance_applied_data reg declarations (5023–5024) and every assignment to them (5051–5052, 5069–5070, 5073–5074, 5098–5099 — the for loop body at 5067–5076 becomes empty and the loop may go with it); declare wire [DESIGN_SIZE*DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data; so that line 5107 still works; add wire norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress); and a generate loop of 16 mult_add_int_18x19 instances wired as in MAPPING, driving assign variance_applied_data[gi*8 +: 8] = res[7:0];. Leave cycle_count, norm_in_progress, done_norm_internal, out_data_available_internal, in_data_available_flopped, inp_data_flopped and the enable/mux logic untouched.

In activation: delete the slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed declarations (5433, 5444) and every assignment to them (5467, 5470, 5482, 5484, 5522, 5525); change intercept_applied_data_internal from reg to wire and delete its assignments (5468, 5485, 5523). Keep line 5483 (data_intercept_flopped <= data_intercept) and the data_intercept_flopped clears at 5471 and 5526 as they are — the tanh branch of the for loop keeps only that one statement, the ReLU branch (5487) is untouched. Add wire act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress); and a generate loop of 16 mult_add_int_18x19 instances wired as in MAPPING, driving assign intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*8 +: 8] = res[7:0];. Line 5534 and everything else stay as written.

Connect every input port by name on all 32 instances, including mode_sigs(12'b0), scanin(19'b0) and chainin(64'b0); leave chainout() and scanout() unconnected.

ASSUMES: inv_var and validity_mask are stable while norm is processing a matrix. The block registers ay at the same edge as ax (one edge before result), so the multiplier it applies is inv_var/validity_mask[i] sampled one cycle earlier than the design samples them at line 5070, where they are read combinationally at the edge that loads variance_applied_data. They agree whenever the two signals do not change during a run. Support in the design: both come from cfg registers (inv_var written only at REG_INV_VAR_ADDR, line 4889; validity_mask_a_rows only at REG_VALID_MASK_A_ROWS_ADDR, line 4894) that top wires straight to norm (lines 5897, 5902), while norm only processes between in_data_available/norm_in_progress (line 5059) and done_norm_internal (5090), a window control enters only after start_tpu and done_mat_mul (lines 5259, 5271, 5276) — i.e. after software has finished configuring. Outside that window norm_blk_reset is high and every block register is 0, exactly as the design's registers are (5051–5052, 5098–5099), so the sampling offset is invisible there.

ASSUMES: activation_type is stable while activation is enabled and processing. When activation_type==0 the design holds slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed and intercept_applied_data_internal (the for loop at 5480–5489 assigns only relu_applied_data_internal in that case), while the block has no clock enable and keeps loading. The held values are only ever read through out_data_internal when activation_type==1 (line 5534), so the two differ only if activation_type changes from 1 to 0 and back within one activation run. Support in the design: activation_type is a cfg register written only at REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR (line 4913) and reset to 1 (line 4838), and activation only runs between in_data_available/activation_in_progress (5477) and done_activation_internal (5505/5513), a window control enters only after start_tpu and the earlier stages complete (5259, 5319). Outside that window act_blk_reset is high and every block register is 0, matching the design's clears at 5467–5469 and 5522–5524, so the missing hold is invisible there. Note the block's reset is deliberately not gated by activation_type: leaving it ungated keeps the 0→1 transition exact (the design loads a fresh product at that edge, and so does the block).

DESIGN_CHANGE: Two small changes in the source would remove both assumptions and make these rewrites unconditional. (1) In norm, latch inv_var and validity_mask into the pipeline one stage earlier — i.e. multiply by a copy of inv_var registered alongside mean_applied_data, and select the pass-through lane with the mask registered at the same edge — so the multiplier's two operands are sampled at the same edge, which is what the block does. (2) In activation, make the activation_type branch at line 5481 select only the output mux (line 5534) and let the slope/intercept pipeline run unconditionally (its registers are already cleared whenever the block is idle), removing the hold the block cannot reproduce. Separately, the 256 seq_mac multipliers can never use this block on this device: at one DSP tile per block they need 256 of the 132 tiles, and their accumulator feeds back through soft logic that chainin cannot carry.

Simulation

simulation passed: 4577 cycles, 0 mismatches. The result (sim_result.json) and log (sim_run.log) are attached. Simulation shows that every output matches on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles); it does not show that the edit follows the plan or the correctness rules. That is your check.

The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v, sim_result.json, sim_run.log

response

I'll read the source and reference files before giving a verdict.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the block contract, and the correctness rules.

Plan conformance

  • Only the two regions the plan names are touched: norm (5021–5123) and activation (5446–5569). Greps confirm no other mult_add_int_18x19 instantiation anywhere in the file, and the deleted names (mean_applied_data, slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed) appear nowhere outside the explanatory comments, so there is no implicit-wire reader left behind.
  • The edit matches the editor notes exactly: declarations and assignments deleted as listed, variance_applied_data and intercept_applied_data_internal turned into wires, data_intercept_flopped <= data_intercept (5495) and its clears (5482, 5533) kept, the ReLU branch (5497), cycle_count, *_in_progress, done_*, out_data_available_internal, in_data_available_flopped, inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped and the LUT/address logic untouched. The now-unused reg [31:0] i; left in norm is harmless and within the minimal edit.

Ports — all eight inputs connected by name on both instances (clk, reset, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax, ay, bx, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0); chainout()/scanout() unconnected, so nothing reads chainout through general routing. Widths are exact: {10'b0,[7:0]}=18, {11'b0,[7:0]}=19 and 19'd1, {28'b0,[7:0]}=36. 32 instances ≤ 132 DSP tiles.

Behaviour — activation: block regs at edge k‑1 hold P,Q,J; result at edge k = P·Q + J, which is the original slope_applied(k-1) + data_intercept_delayed(k-1); the bx path reproduces the data_intercept_flopped → data_intercept_delayed delay (verified across the first two loading edges after a clear, where both give 0 then P·Q). Truncation to [7:0] is the same value mod 256 as the design's 8-bit regs. norm: ax takes over mean_applied_data at the same edge (so mean/inp_data sampling is unshifted), ay=1 reproduces the pass-through lane.

Reset/power-up — act_blk_reset/norm_blk_reset are the exact complements of the two always blocks' load conditions (5488 / 5056), so every design clear (5481–5486, 5532–5537; 5050–5053, 5087–5090) coincides with a block clear, including mid-run; the sim's midrun_resets: 1 exercises this. Both modules receive top-level reset, so all block registers are cleared during the testbench's 4-cycle reset phase; x_count: 0, and no result is relied on before the second edge.

Visibility — the three absorbed register arrays had no readers outside their always blocks; intercept_applied_data_internal (5569) and variance_applied_data (5123) remain visible on result. The four (sum_in=top) norm lanes are converted as products only; pool's four-term sum (5408) stays in soft logic.

The two sampling-offset assumptions (inv_var/validity_mask in norm, activation_type in activation) are stated in the plan with design-line support, and the simulation matches the reference on the testbench stimulus regardless.

output
1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule

prompt

3 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Measured result of your plan

Every candidate tried so far in this run, in order. Each row gives the candidate's figures at seed 1, which every measured candidate was measured at, and then, where more seeds were run, the median over the seeds named, which is the figure the loop compared. The period is register to register (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem). The product is the run's objective, area × delay; the ratios are against the baseline's figures (the median of five seeds, 1 to 5; A/A0 area, D/D0 period, and their product). The original design is the result to beat: the run holds it until a rewrite's area × delay is below the original's, and at the end keeps a rewrite only if its median over every seed is still below.
