1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule

1	    // tpu_like.small.ws top (from the hand-written testbench of 2026-10-19): the design driven as its
2	    // software drives it. Matrices written into the two BRAMs through the external ports (word k of A at
3	    // address_mat_a + k, byte i = A[i][k]; word k of B at address_mat_b + k, byte j = B[k][j]; this
4	    // design's address strides reset to 1), the register block configured over APB, start_tpu written,
5	    // done_tpu polled, the 18 words around address_mat_c read back from BRAM A. A multiply writes its
6	    // results once per reset (the accumulator's start_pooling is cleared by resetn alone, the activation
7	    // unit's done count by reset alone), so every run after the first follows a 4-cycle reset of both.
8	    reg [7:0] MA [0:767]; reg [7:0] MB [0:767];

11	    task fill_matrices; integer q; begin
12	        for (q = 0; q < 768; q = q + 1) begin draw; MA[q] = r32[7:0]; end
13	        for (q = 0; q < 768; q = q + 1) begin draw; MB[q] = r32[7:0]; end
14	    end endtask

17	        tick; PENABLE = 1;
18	        tick; PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0;
19	        tick;
20	    end endtask

29	        tick; bram_we_a_ext = 0;

prompt
Task

Edit tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$470, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$474, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$478, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$482, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$486, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$490, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$494, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$498, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$502, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$506, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$510, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$514, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$518, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$522, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$526, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$530, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$368(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$371(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$374(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$377(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$380, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$383, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$386, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$389, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$392, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$395, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$398, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$401, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$404, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$407, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$410, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$413. The plan names their shared sum as 'NONE (no converted product shares a sum with another; each activation block carries its own intercept on bx, each norm block has no addend)'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
  • The sum these products feed also adds terms that are not products: data_intercept_delayed.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the source to understand the current state and find where the critical path now runs.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$470, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$474, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$478, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$482, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$486, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$490, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$494, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$498, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$502, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$506, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$510, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$514, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$518, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$522, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$526, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$530, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$368(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$371(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$374(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$377(sum_in=top), norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$380, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$383, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$386, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$389, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$392, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$395, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$398, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$401, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$404, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$407, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$410, norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5070$413
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (no converted product shares a sum with another; each activation block carries its own intercept on bx, each norm block has no addend)
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING: 16 blocks in activation (lane i = 0..15, unchanged from the kept candidate): clk=clk, reset=act_blk_reset, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0, inp_data_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, ay={11'b0, data_slope_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, bx={28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result[7:0] -> intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8 +: 8] (a wire), chainout/scanout unconnected. 16 blocks in norm (lane i = 0..15): clk=clk, reset=norm_blk_reset, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0, (inp_data[i*8 +: 8] + lane_offset_i)} where lane_offset_i is a new per-lane 8-bit register holding validity_mask[i] ? (8'd0 - mean) : 8'd0, ay={11'b0, lane_mult_i} where lane_mult_i is a new per-lane 8-bit register holding validity_mask[i] ? inv_var : 8'd1, bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result[7:0] -> variance_applied_data[i*8 +: 8] (a wire), chainout/scanout unconnected.
PLAN:

What changes relative to the measured candidate

Same 32 blocks, same regions, same reset derivation. The one change is the logic in front of the norm blocks' ax and ay ports, which is where the measured critical path ended:

<source reg> -> lNOT -> $sub (inp_data - mean) -> $mux (validity_mask) -> norm block ax[1]   = 5.589 ns

Four routing hops (0.868 + 1.626 + 0.973 + 1.627 = 5.09 ns of the 5.59 ns) and only ~0.4 ns of logic. The inverter and the mask mux are removed from that path by precomputing both mask-dependent, configuration-dependent quantities into per-lane registers one cycle ahead, leaving a single 8-bit add between inp_data and ax:

  • inp_data[i] - mean becomes inp_data[i] + lane_offset_i with lane_offset_i = registered (8'd0 - mean), so no inverter and no mean route on the data path;
  • the validity_mask[i] mux on the stage-1 value disappears entirely: it is folded into lane_offset_i (0 when the lane is masked off, so the add passes inp_data through) and into lane_mult_i (8'd1 when masked off, so the block multiplies by 1);
  • ay is now a register output straight into the port instead of a mux output.

Expected path after the change: source register -> one hop -> 8-bit add -> one hop -> ax, about 3.4 ns, with the two new register arrays placeable next to the blocks. No new multiplier and no extra DSP tile is introduced: the alternative of folding the mean into bx as -(mean*inv_var) was rejected because it creates a fresh 8x8 multiply path from the cfg registers to all 16 blocks, which on this placement (≈1.6 ns per hop) could land above the 5.56 ns it is meant to beat.

Area: 16 8-bit subtractors and 32 8-bit muxes are replaced by 16 8-bit adders and 256 flip-flops, so the logic area is about neutral; the DSP count stays at 32 blocks.

Converted regions
A. activation, the 16 tanh lanes (ids $470 … $530) — unchanged from the measured candidate

Source lines 5450–5458 and 5465–5534. Per lane i, while the block is loading with activation_type==1, the design computes at edge k:
slope_applied_data_internal[i] <= data_slope_flopped[i]*inp_data_flopped[i] (5482), data_intercept_delayed[i] <= data_intercept_flopped[i] (5484), intercept_applied_data_internal[i] <= slope_applied_data_internal[i] + data_intercept_delayed[i] (5485), all 8-bit, i.e. modulo 256.

The block fed ax = inp_data_flopped[i], ay = data_slope_flopped[i], bx = data_intercept_flopped[i] registers those at edge k-1 and loads result at edge k with the same product-plus-intercept; result[7:0] is exactly the 8-bit value the design registers, because every operand is zero-extended and the design truncates at the same point. The block's operand registers take over slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed; the block's result register takes over intercept_applied_data_internal. Those three arrays are deleted. data_intercept_flopped (5483, plus its clears at 5471 and 5526) stays in soft logic, as do inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped, relu_applied_data_internal, cycle_count, activation_in_progress, the done/valid logic and the slope/intercept LUTs and address logic.

Visibility: slope_applied_data_internal is read only at 5485 and data_intercept_delayed only at 5485, so both may live inside the block; intercept_applied_data_internal is read at 5534 and is produced on the result port, so it stays visible.

Reset: act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress) — the exact complement of the load condition of the always block at 5465–5520 (5466 clears, 5477 loads, 5521 clears). Whenever the design clears those three arrays to 0 (5467/5468/5470, 5522/5523/5525) the block clears every one of its registers, and on the first loading edge after a clear the block computes 0*0+0 = 0, matching the design's 0 + 0.

B. norm, the 16 lanes (ids $368 … $413) — multiply plus both register stages into one block each, with the mean fold rewritten

Source lines 5049–5107. Per lane i the design computes at edge k-1 mean_applied_data[i] <= mask[i] ? (inp_data[i]-mean) : inp_data[i] (5069/5073) and at edge k variance_applied_data[i] <= mask[i] ? (mean_applied_data[i]*inv_var) : mean_applied_data[i] (5070/5074), both 8-bit.

The block's ax register takes over mean_applied_data[i], the block's result register takes over variance_applied_data[i]. The two mask choices are folded into the two new per-lane registers:

  • mask = 1: ax = (inp_data[i] + (8'd0 - mean)) mod 256 = (inp_data[i] - mean) mod 256 (identical to 5069), ay = inv_var, so result[7:0] = (mean_applied*inv_var) mod 256 (identical to 5070).
  • mask = 0: lane_offset_i = 0, so ax = inp_data[i] (identical to 5073); lane_mult_i = 1, so result[7:0] = mean_applied (identical to the pass-through of 5074).

bx and chainin are tied to 0: this product has no addend in norm.

Visibility: mean_applied_data is read only at 5070 and 5074 inside the same always block, so it may live inside the block as the ax register; variance_applied_data is read at 5107 (out_data_internal -> out_data -> pool, including the four lanes whose bytes feed pool's window-4 adder at 5392) and is produced on the result port, so it stays visible. The four (sum_in=top) lanes are converted as products only; their shared four-term sum in pool stays in soft logic, unchanged — four distinct products cannot be folded into one block's single bx/chainin, and chainout may not reach general routing.

Reset: norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress) — the exact complement of the load condition at 5050/5059. Every branch of that always block either loads both arrays or clears both to 0 (5051/5052, 5098/5099), so the block's synchronous clear reproduces the register behaviour exactly, including after a mid-run reset. On the first loading edge after a clear the block computes 0*ay + 0 = 0, matching variance_applied_data <= 0 * inv_var.

Power-up: both new register arrays carry a synchronous clear on reset, and all 32 blocks are held in reset through the testbench's 4-cycle reset phase, so every block register is cleared at the first clock edge and no X reaches an output on or after the first compared cycle. No rewrite relies on a block result before its second edge after power-up.

Candidates left unconverted
  • qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances — not implementable on this device. One block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone, so the region needs 256 of the device's 132 tiles. A partial conversion is not proposed either: it would spend up to 132 tiles to remove at most half of the PE multipliers, and the accumulation out_temp <= out_temp + mul_out_temp_reg (4697–4704) is a feedback loop through soft logic that cannot enter the block, since chainin may only be driven by another block's chainout or a constant — so only the multiply, never the accumulate, could move.
  • No other candidate is left: all 16 activation ids and all 16 norm ids are converted.
Register stages, before and after
  • Each activation id: before — 2 stages from the product's operands (inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped, data_intercept_flopped) to the sum result: slope_applied_data_internal/data_intercept_delayed (stage 1), intercept_applied_data_internal (stage 2). After — 2 stages: the block's operand registers (stage 1), the block's result register (stage 2). Unchanged latency; the upstream stage (inp_data_flopped/data_slope_flopped) is untouched.
  • Each norm id: before — 2 stages from inp_data/mean/inv_var to variance_applied_data: mean_applied_data (stage 1), variance_applied_data (stage 2). After — 2 stages: the block's operand registers (stage 1, holding the already-offset lane value), the block's result register (stage 2). Unchanged latency. The new lane_offset/lane_mult registers are not a data-path stage: they hold configuration values and feed the same stage-1 edge.
Edit notes for the editor

In norm:

  1. Delete the mean_applied_data and variance_applied_data reg declarations (5023–5024) and every assignment to them (5051–5052, 5069–5070, 5073–5074, 5098–5099 — the for loop body at 5067–5076 becomes empty and the loop goes with it; reg [31:0] i; at 5048 may stay, unused).
  2. Declare wire [DESIGN_SIZE*DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data; so line 5107 still works.
  3. Add wire norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress);
  4. Add the generate loop (DWIDTH is 8, DESIGN_SIZE is 16):
verilog
genvar gi;
generate
for (gi = 0; gi < `DESIGN_SIZE; gi = gi + 1) begin : norm_dsp
    //The mask choice and the mean negation are configuration-only values; holding
    //them in registers keeps everything but one 8-bit add out of the path to ax.
    reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] lane_offset;
    reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] lane_mult;
    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            lane_offset <= 8'd0;
            lane_mult   <= 8'd0;
        end else begin
            lane_offset <= validity_mask[gi] ? (8'd0 - mean) : 8'd0;
            lane_mult   <= validity_mask[gi] ? inv_var       : 8'd1;
        end
    end
    wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] lane_ax = inp_data[gi*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] + lane_offset;
    wire [63:0] res;
    mult_add_int_18x19 u_norm_mult (
        .clk(clk),
        .reset(norm_blk_reset),
        .mode_sigs(12'b0),
        .ax({10'b0, lane_ax}),
        .ay({11'b0, lane_mult}),
        .bx(36'b0),
        .chainin(64'b0),
        .scanin(19'b0),
        .result(res),
        .chainout(),
        .scanout()
    );
    assign variance_applied_data[gi*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] = res[`DWIDTH-1:0];
end
endgenerate

lane_ax is 8 bits wide, so the add truncates at 8 bits exactly as line 5069 does. Leave cycle_count, norm_in_progress, done_norm_internal, out_data_available_internal, in_data_available_flopped, inp_data_flopped and the enable muxes (5031–5033) untouched.

In activation (identical to the measured candidate):

  1. Delete the slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed declarations (5433, 5444) and every assignment to them (5467, 5470, 5482, 5484, 5522, 5525).
  2. Change intercept_applied_data_internal from reg to wire and delete its assignments (5468, 5485, 5523).
  3. Keep line 5483 (data_intercept_flopped <= data_intercept) and the data_intercept_flopped clears at 5471 and 5526; the tanh branch of the for loop keeps only that one statement, and the ReLU branch (5487) is untouched.
  4. Add wire act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress); and a generate loop of 16 instances wired as in MAPPING, each driving assign intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*DWIDTH +: DWIDTH] = res[DWIDTH-1:0];. Line 5534 and everything else stay as written. Use a generate-block label distinct from the one in norm`.

Connect every input port by name on all 32 instances, including mode_sigs(12'b0), scanin(19'b0) and chainin(64'b0); leave chainout() and scanout() unconnected.

ASSUMES: mean, inv_var and validity_mask are stable while norm is processing a matrix. lane_offset/lane_mult are registered, so the block applies mean, inv_var and the mask as sampled one edge before the block's operand edge, whereas the design reads mean/the mask at the edge that loads mean_applied_data (5069/5073) and inv_var/the mask at the next edge (5070/5074). They agree whenever the three signals do not change during a run. Support in the design: all three come from cfg registers (mean written only at REG_MEAN_ADDR, line 4888; inv_var only at REG_INV_VAR_ADDR, 4889; validity_mask_a_rows only at REG_VALID_MASK_A_ROWS_ADDR, 4894) that top wires straight to norm (5896, 5897, 5902), while norm only processes between in_data_available/norm_in_progress (5059) and done_norm_internal (5090), a window control enters only after start_tpu and done_mat_mul (5259, 5271, 5276) — i.e. after software has finished configuring. Outside that window norm_blk_reset is high and every block register is 0, exactly as the design's registers are (5051–5052, 5098–5099), so the sampling offset is invisible there.

ASSUMES: activation_type is stable while activation is enabled and processing. When activation_type==0 the design holds slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed and intercept_applied_data_internal (the loop at 5480–5489 assigns only relu_applied_data_internal), while the block has no clock enable and keeps loading. The held values are only ever read through out_data_internal when activation_type==1 (5534), so the two differ only if activation_type changes from 1 to 0 and back within one activation run. Support in the design: activation_type is a cfg register written only at REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR (4913), reset to 1 (4838), and activation only runs between in_data_available/activation_in_progress (5477) and done_activation_internal (5505/5513), a window control enters only after start_tpu and the earlier stages complete (5259, 5319). Outside that window act_blk_reset is high and every block register is 0, matching the design's clears at 5467–5469 and 5522–5524. The block's reset is deliberately not gated by activation_type: leaving it ungated keeps the 0->1 transition exact, since the design loads a fresh product at that edge and so does the block.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
// inputs).
// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
// X until the first clear.
module mult_add_int_18x19 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
    input [17:0] ax,
    input [18:0] ay,
    input [35:0] bx,
    input [63:0] chainin,
    input [18:0] scanin,
    output reg [63:0] result,
    output [63:0] chainout,
    output [18:0] scanout
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            result <= 0;
            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
        end
    end

    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
    assign scanout  = ay_reg;

endmodule
===

Bash completed cat /work/sim_reference/drive_tpu_os_top.vh; echo "=== TASKS ==="; cat /work/sim_reference/tasks_tpu…
input
{
 "command": "cat /work/sim_reference/drive_tpu_os_top.vh; echo \"=== TASKS ===\"; cat /work/sim_reference/tasks_tpu_os_top.vh"

output
        // tpu_like.small.os.fixed at its top: the ws drive sequence on the os register map and its stride-16
        // layout. Run 1 matmul alone; the APB write that pulses pe_reset (the design's own one-cycle pulse,
        // with the array quiet: the protocol); a full reset; run 2 every block; a full reset; run 3 matmul
        // and norm; the random phase (APB traffic on the register set except the start/done word, BRAM
        // writes, reset 1 in 1024). Under START_ONLY the full resets and the random resets are left out.
        // Under PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT (the diagnostic) one more APB write of the pe_reset bit lands while run
        // 2's multiply is in flight, which the protocol excludes.
        fill_matrices;
        phase = "run1";
        load_matrices(0, 10'd0, 10'd0);
        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
        apb_write(8'h00, 32'h1); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
        phase = "readback1"; read_c(10'd768, "run 1 (matmul)");
        phase = "pe_reset"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // the pe_reset bit, array quiet
        zero_cycles(4);
        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
        phase = "run2";
        load_matrices(1, 10'd256, 10'd256);
        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
        config_blocks(32'hf); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
`ifdef PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT
        zero_cycles(30); phase = "pe_reset-live"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // a one-cycle pe_reset with products in flight
`endif
        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
        phase = "readback2"; read_c(10'd768, "run 2 (every block)");
        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
        phase = "run3";
        load_matrices(2, 10'd512, 10'd512);
        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
        config_blocks(32'h3); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
        phase = "readback3"; read_c(10'd768, "run 3 (matmul and norm, after reset)");
        phase = "random"; ph = 0;
        for (i = 0; i < `RANDOM_CYCLES; i = i + 1) begin
            tick; set_data;
            case (ph)
                0: begin PSEL = 1; draw; PWRITE = r32[0]; PADDR = reg_addr(r32[5:1]); if (PADDR == 8'h04) PADDR = 8'h00; PENABLE = 0; apb_addr = PADDR; apb_write_q = PWRITE; apb_wdata = PWDATA; end   // no random start or pe_reset write: the protocol
                1: begin PSEL = 1; PENABLE = 1; PADDR = apb_addr; PWRITE = apb_write_q; PWDATA = apb_wdata; end   // set_data redrew them; the access cycle keeps the setup cycle's (APB: stable through setup and access)
                2: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; end
                default: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; end
            endcase
            ph = (ph + 1) % 4;
            draw; bram_we_a_ext = (r32[1:0] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0; bram_we_b_ext = (r32[3:2] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0;
            draw; reset = (`MIDRUN_RESETS && (r32 % 1024) == 0); resetn = ~reset;
        end
        PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; bram_we_a_ext = 0; bram_we_b_ext = 0;
=== TASKS ===
    // tpu_like.small.ws top (from the hand-written testbench of 2026-10-19): the design driven as its
    // software drives it. Matrices written into the two BRAMs through the external ports (word k of A at
    // address_mat_a + k, byte i = A[i][k]; word k of B at address_mat_b + k, byte j = B[k][j]; this
    // design's address strides reset to 1), the register block configured over APB, start_tpu written,
    // done_tpu polled, the 18 words around address_mat_c read back from BRAM A. A multiply writes its
    // results once per reset (the accumulator's start_pooling is cleared by resetn alone, the activation
    // unit's done count by reset alone), so every run after the first follows a 4-cycle reset of both.

prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. (8'd0 - mean) : 8'd0, ay={11'b0, lane_mult_i} where lane_mult_i is a new per-lane 8-bit register holding validity_mask[i] ? inv_var : 8'd1, bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result[7:0] -> variance_applied_data[i*8 +: 8] (a wire), chainout/scanout unconnected.
PLAN:

What changes relative to the measured candidate

Same 32 blocks, same regions, same reset derivation. The one change is the logic in front of the norm blocks' ax and ay ports, which is where the measured critical path ended:

<source reg> -> lNOT -> $sub (inp_data - mean) -> $mux (validity_mask) -> norm block ax[1]   = 5.589 ns

Four routing hops (0.868 + 1.626 + 0.973 + 1.627 = 5.09 ns of the 5.59 ns) and only ~0.4 ns of logic. The inverter and the mask mux are removed from that path by precomputing both mask-dependent, configuration-dependent quantities into per-lane registers one cycle ahead, leaving a single 8-bit add between inp_data and ax:

  • inp_data[i] - mean becomes inp_data[i] + lane_offset_i with lane_offset_i = registered (8'd0 - mean), so no inverter and no mean route on the data path;
  • the validity_mask[i] mux on the stage-1 value disappears entirely: it is folded into lane_offset_i (0 when the lane is masked off, so the add passes inp_data through) and into lane_mult_i (8'd1 when masked off, so the block multiplies by 1);
  • ay is now a register output straight into the port instead of a mux output.

Expected path after the change: source register -> one hop -> 8-bit add -> one hop -> ax, about 3.4 ns, with the two new register arrays placeable next to the blocks. No new multiplier and no extra DSP tile is introduced: the alternative of folding the mean into bx as -(mean*inv_var) was rejected because it creates a fresh 8x8 multiply path from the cfg registers to all 16 blocks, which on this placement (≈1.6 ns per hop) could land above the 5.56 ns it is meant to beat.

Area: 16 8-bit subtractors and 32 8-bit muxes are replaced by 16 8-bit adders and 256 flip-flops, so the logic area is about neutral; the DSP count stays at 32 blocks.

Converted regions
A. activation, the 16 tanh lanes (ids $470 … $530) — unchanged from the measured candidate

Source lines 5450–5458 and 5465–5534. Per lane i, while the block is loading with activation_type==1, the design computes at edge k:
slope_applied_data_internal[i] <= data_slope_flopped[i]*inp_data_flopped[i] (5482), data_intercept_delayed[i] <= data_intercept_flopped[i] (5484), intercept_applied_data_internal[i] <= slope_applied_data_internal[i] + data_intercept_delayed[i] (5485), all 8-bit, i.e. modulo 256.

The block fed ax = inp_data_flopped[i], ay = data_slope_flopped[i], bx = data_intercept_flopped[i] registers those at edge k-1 and loads result at edge k with the same product-plus-intercept; result[7:0] is exactly the 8-bit value the design registers, because every operand is zero-extended and the design truncates at the same point. The block's operand registers take over slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed; the block's result register takes over intercept_applied_data_internal. Those three arrays are deleted. data_intercept_flopped (5483, plus its clears at 5471 and 5526) stays in soft logic, as do inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped, relu_applied_data_internal, cycle_count, activation_in_progress, the done/valid logic and the slope/intercept LUTs and address logic.

Visibility: slope_applied_data_internal is read only at 5485 and data_intercept_delayed only at 5485, so both may live inside the block; intercept_applied_data_internal is read at 5534 and is produced on the result port, so it stays visible.

Reset: act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress) — the exact complement of the load condition of the always block at 5465–5520 (5466 clears, 5477 loads, 5521 clears). Whenever the design clears those three arrays to 0 (5467/5468/5470, 5522/5523/5525) the block clears every one of its registers, and on the first loading edge after a clear the block computes 0*0+0 = 0, matching the design's 0 + 0.

B. norm, the 16 lanes (ids $368 … $413) — multiply plus both register stages into one block each, with the mean fold rewritten

Source lines 5049–5107. Per lane i the design computes at edge k-1 mean_applied_data[i] <= mask[i] ? (inp_data[i]-mean) : inp_data[i] (5069/5073) and at edge k variance_applied_data[i] <= mask[i] ? (mean_applied_data[i]*inv_var) : mean_applied_data[i] (5070/5074), both 8-bit.

The block's ax register takes over mean_applied_data[i], the block's result register takes over variance_applied_data[i]. The two mask choices are folded into the two new per-lane registers:

  • mask = 1: ax = (inp_data[i] + (8'd0 - mean)) mod 256 = (inp_data[i] - mean) mod 256 (identical to 5069), ay = inv_var, so result[7:0] = (mean_applied*inv_var) mod 256 (identical to 5070).
  • mask = 0: lane_offset_i = 0, so ax = inp_data[i] (identical to 5073); lane_mult_i = 1, so result[7:0] = mean_applied (identical to the pass-through of 5074).

bx and chainin are tied to 0: this product has no addend in norm.

Visibility: mean_applied_data is read only at 5070 and 5074 inside the same always block, so it may live inside the block as the ax register; variance_applied_data is read at 5107 (out_data_internal -> out_data -> pool, including the four lanes whose bytes feed pool's window-4 adder at 5392) and is produced on the result port, so it stays visible. The four (sum_in=top) lanes are converted as products only; their shared four-term sum in pool stays in soft logic, unchanged — four distinct products cannot be folded into one block's single bx/chainin, and chainout may not reach general routing.

Reset: norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress) — the exact complement of the load condition at 5050/5059. Every branch of that always block either loads both arrays or clears both to 0 (5051/5052, 5098/5099), so the block's synchronous clear reproduces the register behaviour exactly, including after a mid-run reset. On the first loading edge after a clear the block computes 0*ay + 0 = 0, matching variance_applied_data <= 0 * inv_var.

Power-up: both new register arrays carry a synchronous clear on reset, and all 32 blocks are held in reset through the testbench's 4-cycle reset phase, so every block register is cleared at the first clock edge and no X reaches an output on or after the first compared cycle. No rewrite relies on a block result before its second edge after power-up.

Candidates left unconverted
  • qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances — not implementable on this device. One block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone, so the region needs 256 of the device's 132 tiles. A partial conversion is not proposed either: it would spend up to 132 tiles to remove at most half of the PE multipliers, and the accumulation out_temp <= out_temp + mul_out_temp_reg (4697–4704) is a feedback loop through soft logic that cannot enter the block, since chainin may only be driven by another block's chainout or a constant — so only the multiply, never the accumulate, could move.
  • No other candidate is left: all 16 activation ids and all 16 norm ids are converted.
Register stages, before and after
  • Each activation id: before — 2 stages from the product's operands (inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped, data_intercept_flopped) to the sum result: slope_applied_data_internal/data_intercept_delayed (stage 1), intercept_applied_data_internal (stage 2). After — 2 stages: the block's operand registers (stage 1), the block's result register (stage 2). Unchanged latency; the upstream stage (inp_data_flopped/data_slope_flopped) is untouched.
  • Each norm id: before — 2 stages from inp_data/mean/inv_var to variance_applied_data: mean_applied_data (stage 1), variance_applied_data (stage 2). After — 2 stages: the block's operand registers (stage 1, holding the already-offset lane value), the block's result register (stage 2). Unchanged latency. The new lane_offset/lane_mult registers are not a data-path stage: they hold configuration values and feed the same stage-1 edge.
Edit notes for the editor

In norm:

  1. Delete the mean_applied_data and variance_applied_data reg declarations (5023–5024) and every assignment to them (5051–5052, 5069–5070, 5073–5074, 5098–5099 — the for loop body at 5067–5076 becomes empty and the loop goes with it; reg [31:0] i; at 5048 may stay, unused).
  2. Declare wire [DESIGN_SIZE*DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data; so line 5107 still works.
  3. Add wire norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress);
  4. Add the generate loop (DWIDTH is 8, DESIGN_SIZE is 16):
verilog
genvar gi;
generate
for (gi = 0; gi < `DESIGN_SIZE; gi = gi + 1) begin : norm_dsp
    //The mask choice and the mean negation are configuration-only values; holding
    //them in registers keeps everything but one 8-bit add out of the path to ax.
    reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] lane_offset;
    reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] lane_mult;
    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            lane_offset <= 8'd0;
            lane_mult   <= 8'd0;
        end else begin
            lane_offset <= validity_mask[gi] ? (8'd0 - mean) : 8'd0;
            lane_mult   <= validity_mask[gi] ? inv_var       : 8'd1;
        end
    end
    wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] lane_ax = inp_data[gi*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] + lane_offset;
    wire [63:0] res;
    mult_add_int_18x19 u_norm_mult (
        .clk(clk),
        .reset(norm_blk_reset),
        .mode_sigs(12'b0),
        .ax({10'b0, lane_ax}),
        .ay({11'b0, lane_mult}),
        .bx(36'b0),
        .chainin(64'b0),
        .scanin(19'b0),
        .result(res),
        .chainout(),
        .scanout()
    );
    assign variance_applied_data[gi*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] = res[`DWIDTH-1:0];
end
endgenerate

lane_ax is 8 bits wide, so the add truncates at 8 bits exactly as line 5069 does. Leave cycle_count, norm_in_progress, done_norm_internal, out_data_available_internal, in_data_available_flopped, inp_data_flopped and the enable muxes (5031–5033) untouched.

In activation (identical to the measured candidate):

  1. Delete the slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed declarations (5433, 5444) and every assignment to them (5467, 5470, 5482, 5484, 5522, 5525).
  2. Change intercept_applied_data_internal from reg to wire and delete its assignments (5468, 5485, 5523).
  3. Keep line 5483 (data_intercept_flopped <= data_intercept) and the data_intercept_flopped clears at 5471 and 5526; the tanh branch of the for loop keeps only that one statement, and the ReLU branch (5487) is untouched.
  4. Add wire act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress); and a generate loop of 16 instances wired as in MAPPING, each driving assign intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*DWIDTH +: DWIDTH] = res[DWIDTH-1:0];. Line 5534 and everything else stay as written. Use a generate-block label distinct from the one in norm`.

Connect every input port by name on all 32 instances, including mode_sigs(12'b0), scanin(19'b0) and chainin(64'b0); leave chainout() and scanout() unconnected.

ASSUMES: mean, inv_var and validity_mask are stable while norm is processing a matrix. lane_offset/lane_mult are registered, so the block applies mean, inv_var and the mask as sampled one edge before the block's operand edge, whereas the design reads mean/the mask at the edge that loads mean_applied_data (5069/5073) and inv_var/the mask at the next edge (5070/5074). They agree whenever the three signals do not change during a run. Support in the design: all three come from cfg registers (mean written only at REG_MEAN_ADDR, line 4888; inv_var only at REG_INV_VAR_ADDR, 4889; validity_mask_a_rows only at REG_VALID_MASK_A_ROWS_ADDR, 4894) that top wires straight to norm (5896, 5897, 5902), while norm only processes between in_data_available/norm_in_progress (5059) and done_norm_internal (5090), a window control enters only after start_tpu and done_mat_mul (5259, 5271, 5276) — i.e. after software has finished configuring. Outside that window norm_blk_reset is high and every block register is 0, exactly as the design's registers are (5051–5052, 5098–5099), so the sampling offset is invisible there.

ASSUMES: activation_type is stable while activation is enabled and processing. When activation_type==0 the design holds slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed and intercept_applied_data_internal (the loop at 5480–5489 assigns only relu_applied_data_internal), while the block has no clock enable and keeps loading. The held values are only ever read through out_data_internal when activation_type==1 (5534), so the two differ only if activation_type changes from 1 to 0 and back within one activation run. Support in the design: activation_type is a cfg register written only at REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR (4913), reset to 1 (4838), and activation only runs between in_data_available/activation_in_progress (5477) and done_activation_internal (5505/5513), a window control enters only after start_tpu and the earlier stages complete (5259, 5319). Outside that window act_blk_reset is high and every block register is 0, matching the design's clears at 5467–5469 and 5522–5524. The block's reset is deliberately not gated by activation_type: leaving it ungated keeps the 0->1 transition exact, since the design loads a fresh product at that edge and so does the block.

DESIGN_CHANGE: Three changes in the source would remove the assumptions and open more of the design to this block. (1) In norm, latch mean, inv_var and validity_mask into the pipeline one stage earlier, so the multiplier's operands and the mask are sampled at the same edge the block samples them. (2) In activation, make the activation_type branch at 5481 select only the output mux at 5534 and let the slope/intercept pipeline run unconditionally, removing a hold the block cannot reproduce. (3) The 256 seq_mac multipliers can never use this block on this device: at one DSP tile per block they need 256 of the 132 tiles, and their accumulator feeds back through soft logic that chainin cannot carry; breaking the PE accumulation into a chain of blocks (one chainout to the next chainin) rather than a soft feedback loop would be the only route. Separately, the remaining long paths in this design are outside the block's reach: the worst is the activation tanh address logic (5580–5619), eleven range comparisons and a priority encode between the pooling output register and data_slope_flopped, which no rewrite onto this block can shorten.

response

I'll start by reading the edited source and the simulation artifacts.

Now the edited source — the norm module region first.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the block contract and the correctness rules.

Plan conformance — the edit touches only norm (5021–5138) and activation (5461–5586), the two plan regions. 16 + 16 = 32 blocks (norm_dsp/act_dsp generate loops over DESIGN_SIZE=16), matching BLOCKS: 32, within the 132 DSP tiles. Every deletion listed in the edit notes is present and nothing else changed: mean_applied_data/variance_applied_data assignments and the for loop gone from norm, slope_applied_data_internal/data_intercept_delayed/intercept_applied_data_internal assignments gone from activation, while data_intercept_flopped (tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5512) and its clears (5499, 5550), relu_applied_data_internal (5514), inp_data_flopped/data_slope_flopped (5481–5489) and all control logic stay. The new lane_offset/lane_mult registers (5108–5118) are exactly the mean/mask fold the plan specifies.

Ports — all eight inputs connected by name on both instances with correct widths: mode_sigs(12'b0), ax 10+8=18, ay 11+8=19, bx 36 (36'b0 in norm, 28+8 in activation), chainin(64'b0), scanin(19'b0), plus clk/reset. chainout()/scanout() unconnected, nothing reads them — consistent with the dedicated-chain restriction. result[7:0] drives variance_applied_data (5134) and intercept_applied_data_internal (5582), as MAPPING says.

Same behavior — activation: design stage 1 is ds_f(k⁻)*inp_f(k⁻) and di_delayed=di_f(k⁻), stage 2 their sum, both mod 256; the block's operand registers hold the same three values at edge k and result[7:0] at edge k+1 is (ax*ay+bx) mod 256 — identical truncation point, identical 2-stage latency. Norm: lane_ax is 8 bits, so inp_data + (8'd0-mean) truncates exactly as inp_data - mean at 5069 of the original; mask=0 gives offset 0 and multiplier 1, reproducing both pass-throughs.

Visibility — grep confirms no remaining reader of mean_applied_data, slope_applied_data_internal or data_intercept_delayed anywhere in the file; the two values that are read elsewhere (variance_applied_data at 5138, intercept_applied_data_internal at 5586) come out on result.

Reset — norm_blk_reset (5100) and act_blk_reset (5563) are the exact complements of the load conditions at 5051/5058 and 5497/5505, so every edge on which the design cleared those arrays clears all block registers, and the first loading edge after a clear yields 0·0+0 = 0 in both. The added lane_* registers carry a synchronous clear on reset, so nothing relies on an unknown initial value; the one residual corner (norm loading at the very first edge after reset deassertion, where lane_mult would still be 0) is unreachable — in_data_available is matmul_c_data_available (5950), produced by the matmul pipeline well after start_tpu.

Assumptions — both ASSUMES: lines are stated with design citations, and they cover the only real deviations: the one-edge-earlier sampling of mean/inv_var/validity_mask, and the activation_type hold the block cannot reproduce. Sim (4577 cycles, 0 mismatches, 1 mid-run reset, 0 X) is consistent with this.

Read completed /work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v"
}
output
1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule
57

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: no gain beyond noise
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite

kept
Pass 1
loop stopped by
no gain beyond noise (gain -4.95% against first, min-gain 0.0345 (baseline seed_figures (area x delay over the entry's seed runs)))
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 6.2834e+07 · critical path 6.03149 ns · wirelength 395418 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 3.78983e+08 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 3.45%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 3.45%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

Pass 1 · kept product 3.64639e+08 (-3.78%)
tier
T2
plan header
BLOCKS 32 · TILES · STAGE_NS
assumptions
`inv_var` and `validity_mask` are stable while `norm` is processing a matrix. The block registers `ay` at the same edge as `ax` (one edge before `result`), so the multiplier it applies is `inv_var`/`validity_mask[i]` sampled one cycle earlier than the design samples them at line 5070, where they are read combinationally at the edge that loads `variance_applied_data`. They agree whenever the two signals do not change during a run. Support in the design: both come from `cfg` registers (`inv_var` written only at `REG_INV_VAR_ADDR`, line 4889; `validity_mask_a_rows` only at `REG_VALID_MASK_A_ROWS_ADDR`, line 4894) that `top` wires straight to `norm` (lines 5897, 5902), while `norm` only processes between `in_data_available`/`norm_in_progress` (line 5059) and `done_norm_internal` (5090), a window `control` enters only after `start_tpu` and `done_mat_mul` (lines 5259, 5271, 5276) — i.e. after software has finished configuring. Outside that window `norm_blk_reset` is high and every block register is 0, exactly as the design's registers are (5051–5052, 5098–5099), so the sampling offset is invisible there.
`activation_type` is stable while `activation` is enabled and processing. When `activation_type==0` the design holds `slope_applied_data_internal`, `data_intercept_delayed` and `intercept_applied_data_internal` (the `for` loop at 5480–5489 assigns only `relu_applied_data_internal` in that case), while the block has no clock enable and keeps loading. The held values are only ever read through `out_data_internal` when `activation_type==1` (line 5534), so the two differ only if `activation_type` changes from 1 to 0 and back within one activation run. Support in the design: `activation_type` is a `cfg` register written only at `REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR` (line 4913) and reset to 1 (line 4838), and `activation` only runs between `in_data_available`/`activation_in_progress` (5477) and `done_activation_internal` (5505/5513), a window `control` enters only after `start_tpu` and the earlier stages complete (5259, 5319). Outside that window `act_blk_reset` is high and every block register is 0, matching the design's clears at 5467–5469 and 5522–5524, so the missing hold is invisible there. Note the block's reset is deliberately *not* gated by `activation_type`: leaving it ungated keeps the 0→1 transition exact (the design loads a fresh product at that edge, and so does the block).

Pass 2 product 3.72429e+08 (-1.73%)
tier
T2
plan header
BLOCKS 32 · TILES · STAGE_NS
assumptions
`mean`, `inv_var` and `validity_mask` are stable while `norm` is processing a matrix. `lane_offset`/`lane_mult` are registered, so the block applies `mean`, `inv_var` and the mask as sampled one edge before the block's operand edge, whereas the design reads `mean`/the mask at the edge that loads `mean_applied_data` (5069/5073) and `inv_var`/the mask at the next edge (5070/5074). They agree whenever the three signals do not change during a run. Support in the design: all three come from `cfg` registers (`mean` written only at `REG_MEAN_ADDR`, line 4888; `inv_var` only at `REG_INV_VAR_ADDR`, 4889; `validity_mask_a_rows` only at `REG_VALID_MASK_A_ROWS_ADDR`, 4894) that `top` wires straight to `norm` (5896, 5897, 5902), while `norm` only processes between `in_data_available`/`norm_in_progress` (5059) and `done_norm_internal` (5090), a window `control` enters only after `start_tpu` and `done_mat_mul` (5259, 5271, 5276) — i.e. after software has finished configuring. Outside that window `norm_blk_reset` is high and every block register is 0, exactly as the design's registers are (5051–5052, 5098–5099), so the sampling offset is invisible there.
`activation_type` is stable while `activation` is enabled and processing. When `activation_type==0` the design holds `slope_applied_data_internal`, `data_intercept_delayed` and `intercept_applied_data_internal` (the loop at 5480–5489 assigns only `relu_applied_data_internal`), while the block has no clock enable and keeps loading. The held values are only ever read through `out_data_internal` when `activation_type==1` (5534), so the two differ only if `activation_type` changes from 1 to 0 and back within one activation run. Support in the design: `activation_type` is a `cfg` register written only at `REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR` (4913), reset to 1 (4838), and `activation` only runs between `in_data_available`/`activation_in_progress` (5477) and `done_activation_internal` (5505/5513), a window `control` enters only after `start_tpu` and the earlier stages complete (5259, 5319). Outside that window `act_blk_reset` is high and every block register is 0, matching the design's clears at 5467–5469 and 5522–5524. The block's reset is deliberately not gated by `activation_type`: leaving it ungated keeps the 0->1 transition exact, since the design loads a fresh product at that edge and so does the block.

+//The 16 tanh lanes on the hard multiply-add block. The block's operand registers take over
+//slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed; the block's result register takes over
